## L-1-1

N_C(1,1) >= 1. Operation: rank specialization.

Sources: [shapiro-2000](sources.md#shapiro-2000). Chapter 14, p.300.

1. Set x=(1,0,...,0). The induced n by 1 matrix A satisfies A^t A=I_1.
2. Hence rank(A)=1 and n >= 1.

Sources: [dugger-isaksen-2007](sources.md#dugger-isaksen-2007). Theorem 1.2, p.943.

1. If length <= 3 existed, append zero outputs to obtain length 3.

Sources: [dugger-isaksen-2007](sources.md#dugger-isaksen-2007). Theorem 1.2, p.943.

1. If length <= 24 existed, append zero outputs to obtain length 24.
2. 24-9 < 16 < 17, but binomial(24,16)=735471 is odd, contradicting Theorem 1.2.

## L-10-11

N_C(10,11) >= 17. Operation: source restriction and Lam-Lam transfer.

Sources: [shapiro-2000](sources.md#shapiro-2000). Theorem 12.21, p.245; Lemma 14.1, p.300.

1. Restrict a putative [10,11,n] complex formula to (10,11), then orient it as (10,11).
2. Write its complex output as U+iV on real inputs. The identity gives ||U||^2-||V||^2=||x||^2||y||^2, so U is a nonsingular real bilinear map (Lemma 14.1).
3. Theorem 12.21 gives sigma(10,11)=17; sigma(10,11) <= 10 # 11 <= n.

2. Write its complex output as U+iV on real inputs. The identity gives ||U||^2-||V||^2=||x||^2||y||^2, so U is a nonsingular real bilinear map (Lemma 14.1).
3. Theorem 12.21 gives sigma(10,13)=19; sigma(10,13) <= 10 # 13 <= n.

## L-16-16

N_C(16,16) >= 23. Operation: source restriction and Lam-Lam transfer.

Sources: [shapiro-2000](sources.md#shapiro-2000). Theorem 12.21, p.245; Lemma 14.1, p.300.

1. Restrict a putative [16,16,n] complex formula to (11,16), then orient it as (11,16).
2. Write its complex output as U+iV on real inputs. The identity gives ||U||^2-||V||^2=||x||^2||y||^2, so U is a nonsingular real bilinear map (Lemma 14.1).
3. Theorem 12.21 gives sigma(11,16)=23; sigma(11,16) <= 11 # 16 <= n.

## L-25-25

N_C(25,25) >= 38. Operation: Lam-Lam transfer and explicit specialization.

Sources: [antoniano-gitler-1984](sources.md#antoniano-gitler-1984), [shapiro-2000](sources.md#shapiro-2000). Shapiro Lemma 14.1 p.300; Antoniano–Gitler Theorem (1.6) p.6 and exception analysis pp.7–9.

1. A complex formula gives a nonsingular real bilinear map by the Lam–Lam lemma. Projectivizing gives an axial map.
2. Its projective dimensions are (m,n,k)=(24,24,N-1). The exception analysis has n<=15; here n=24>=16.
3. Theorem (1.6) requires 24<2(N-25), hence N>=38.

1. If [27,29,n] existed with n <= 44, pad to 44, restrict and orient to [23,29,44].
2. Set a=11, b=14, m=22, d=3; max(a,b)<m<=a+b and 0<=k=2<=d-floor((d+1)/3).
3. Theorem 1.1 and Proposition 2.15 require 4 | S_2=binomial(23,10)=1144066, but the remainder is 2.

## L-32-32

N_C(32,32) >= 48. Operation: Lam-Lam transfer and explicit specialization.

Sources: [antoniano-gitler-1984](sources.md#antoniano-gitler-1984), [shapiro-2000](sources.md#shapiro-2000). Shapiro Lemma 14.1 p.300; Antoniano–Gitler Theorem (1.6) p.6 and exception analysis pp.7–9.

1. A complex formula gives a nonsingular real bilinear map by the Lam–Lam lemma. Projectivizing gives an axial map.
2. Its projective dimensions are (m,n,k)=(31,31,N-1). The exception analysis has n<=15; here n=31>=16.
3. Theorem (1.6) requires 31<2(N-32), hence N>=48.
